Research collected from outside sources and what this site derives from them; every fact links to a saved copy of its source Research or story?

Every source, every capture, every hash

In plain English Every web page this site relied on, with the time it was saved and a fingerprint proving it has not been altered since.

227 sources, 89 of them primary, 222 dated captures and 1386 hashed files, from the evidence vault at commit f3c5db0d9873. 15 sources could not be read because the site blocked an automated browser; the block, with the challenge page's hash, is the record. The captures are third-party pages held privately and are not republished; this page links to the originals and lists the SHA-256 of every captured file, so that anyone the vault is later opened to can check that the bytes are the ones the agents saw, at the minute they saw them.

PHP October 2026 (php.oct.2026.newsroom.sgit.ai) tracks PHP news for one month. This month that is one story, the two critical PHP security flaws, followed as evidence: every claim with a status and its sources, every source captured, dated and hashed into a private vault. Built and run by one editor and a team of software agents; the roles and the method are public, and the site is rebuilt from the vault, never written by hand. One of the SGit Newsroom's story sites: up from here are October 2026, the year and the newsroom, whose own section on the story is here. Part of the sgit.ai network.

⚠ Participant disclosure: published by the sgit project, which builds the vault and metering stack this site runs on. Content CC BY 4.0 for this site's own text, tables and data; quoted words are their authors', linked; no third-party page is reproduced. Code Apache-2.0.

◉ This page sends one anonymous usage event (page, referrer host, country and region, no IP, no cookie) to a write-only vault lane; what is sent and what is counted. Honoured: Do Not Track and Global Privacy Control.
